Nah. 1. 3. and he will be by no means prejudicial to his justice, if they do not make themselves capable of his mercy, by repentance of sin for the time past, and reformation of life for the time to come.

*{visiting the iniquity}* See Annot. on Exod. 20. 5.

**V. 8.** *{Bowed the head}* Though God be a spirit, and therefore principally to be worshipped in spirit, Joh. 4. 24. yet man has a body as well a spirit: and he must glorify him in both, 1 Cor. 6. 19. for both are his and not only by right of creation, but of redemption also, as the same place of the apostle plainly proves, 1 Cor. 6. 19, 20.

**V. 9.** *{I pray thee}* Moses found grace in God's sight, and he improves all his interest in God's favor, for the people's safety and prays so much more importunately even for forty days and forty nights, as they were more ungrateful towards God, and himself more gracious with God; and he bends, and bows, and humbles himself so much the more, as they were more stiff-necked, and stouthearted.

*{stiff-necked people}* God denies his presence because they were a stiff-necked people, Chap. 33. 3. and Moses desires it upon the same ground: but for different reasons: God because they had made themselves unworthy of such a favor, and by abusing it, might more provoke his anger to their own ruin; Moses desires God's presence, the better to keep them in awe and obedience, who otherwise would be like to be unruly: and therefore seeing the people are of this nature, the rulers have need to call upon God, that he would always be present with his spirit for their assistance, in resistance of their willful rebellions.

*{for thine inheritance}* Psal. 33. 12. Zach. 2. 12.

**V. 10.** *{I make a Covenant}* That is, I renew my covenant already made, or, I bind myself now to do more than I have done; (which was a new covenant) this may be collected from the words following after. Deut. 5. 2.

*{before all thy people}* They are called Moses his people, because committed to his government. See Annot. on Chap. 32. 7.

*{marvails, such}* Both in the wilderness, and in the land of Canaan, as fetching streams of water with a stroke out of the hard rock, Psal. 78. 20. blowing down strong walls with the breath of rams' horns, Josh. 6. 20. making the sun and moon stand still, Josh. 10. 13.

*{with thee}* That is, with your conduct of the people, or on yours and their behalf. See Isai. 63. 11. Mark 16. 20. 1 Corinth. 15. 16.

**V. 11.** *{Amorite}* See Annot. on Gen. 24. 39.

**V. 12.** *{Take heed to thyself}* Lest by making a league with idolaters you become an idolater as they are, and so when they are cast out for their wickedness, you be unworthy to be received or continued in their stead, Chap. 23. 32. Deut. 7. 2.

*{make no Covenant}* Chap. 23. 32. Deut. 7. 2.

*{snare}* Whereby you may be taken with their enticements, and so made partaker of their punishments.

**V. 13.** *{Destroy}* It is not enough to be no idolaters ourselves, but we must be zealous against the idolatry of others, every man according to his interest in God's cause, and the duty of his place whereto God has called him. Chap. 23. 24.

*{groves}* Pleasant shady places which they chose for their idols. See on Gen. 21. 33.

**V. 14.** *{Whose Name}* By name is meant God himself: as, *The Name of the God of Jacob defend thee*, Psal. 20. 1. So the next words (is a jealous God) do show.

*{Jealous}* See the Annot. on Exod. 20. 5.

**V. 15.** *{They go a whoring}* The precedent words seem to be directed to Moses alone in the second person, now the second person is changed into the third: the meaning is, that Moses if he should make such a covenant as a public governor, would include the people in the covenant, and they by that means would be brought to go a-whoring after idols, for idolatry is spiritual whoredom; so in Jer. 2. and 3. Ezek. 16. and elsewhere; and that because there is a kind of matrimonial covenant betwixt God and his people, as Hos. 2. 16. in which respect he is called a jealous God, in the precedent verse, and Chap. 20. 5. and they that transgress this covenant in turning their hearts from God to any creature of the world are guilty of adultery; Jam. 4. 4. Psal. 73. 27. Lev. 17. 7.

**V. 16.** *{Their daughters}* 1 King. 11. 2.

*{make thy sons}* The weaker sex may overcome the stronger, by temptations to idolatry, therefore such unequal marriages are dangerous, and to be declined. See Neh. 13. 25. Num. 25. 1, 2.

**V. 17.** *{No molten gods}* By this all idolatrous representations are forbidden, as by graven images in the second commandment; but the prohibition is here made rather by the name of a molten god, because the golden calf was such a one, Chap. 32. 4.

**V. 18.** *{Unleavened bread}* This and the other particulars, for the most part from hence to ver. 24. are spoken of before, Chap. 23. and have Annot. upon them.

*{month Abib}* Chap. 13. 4.

**V. 19.** *{All that openeth}* That is, every male, Chap. 22. 29. Ezek. 44. 30.

**V. 20.** *{Of an Ass}* See Annot. on Exod. 13. 13.

*{Lamb}* Or, *Kid*.

*{empty}* Without offering something.

**V. 21.** *{six days}* Chap. 23. 12. Deut. 5. 13. Luk. 13. 14.

*{in earing time, and in harvest}* Though tillage for an harvest, and harvest for man's sustenance, be most necessary, yet the religious rest of the Sabbath must not be broken for them, how much less for needless works or carnal sports.

**V. 22.** *{Of weeks}* That is, of seven weeks after the Passover, viz. the feast of Pentecost, Lev. 23. 15. after 49 days, that is, a week of weeks, which is the fiftieth day.

*{year's end}* Heb. *revolution of the year*. See Annot. on Chap. 23. 16.

**V. 23.** *{thrice in the year}* Chap. 23. 14, 17. Deut. 16. 16. See Psalm 84. 7.

**V. 24.** *{desire thy land}* God will not only keep others' hands from spoiling you, but will curb the concupiscence of their hearts from desiring your portion; because they shall have no hope (if they attempt) to possess it, so that you may freely appear, where he appoints you, with assurance to find all things in safety at your return. See Gen. 35. 5. Acts 18. 10.

**V. 25.** *{With leaven}* See Annot. on Chap. 23. 15, 18.

*{until the morning}* See Annot. on Chap. 12. 10.

*{in the mother's milk}* See Annot. on Chap. 23. 19.

**V. 27.** *{These words}* That is the ceremonial precepts, which was accordingly done, when he wrote the book of Exodus; but for the words of the moral law, God wrote them himself twice over, ver. 2. See the second Annot. on ver. 28. of this chapter.

**V. 28.** *{Forty days, &c.}* This remaining in the mount was another forty days from the former, Chap. 24. 18. This miracle was to confirm the authority of the law; and the like is observed of our Savior before his publication of the gospel, who fasted also forty days and forty nights, which both being miraculous, are no more to be followed by men than other miracles are. See Annot. on Exod. 24. 18.

*{he wrote}* Some think God wrote the first writing, and Moses the second, as God seemed to command him, ver. 27. But see 2 Annot. on Chap. 31. 18.

**V. 29.** *{Moses wist not}* That is, knew not, for no man can see his own face, but in a looking glass; or if he had seen it, it would seem nothing to that glory which he saw of God when he passed by.

*{face shone}* Moses desired to see God's glory, Chap. 33. 18. Now he himself has by the reflex of the divine splendor, so much glory upon him that others for fear dare not look upon him, how should he then, or anyone else be able to comprehend the glory of the divine majesty, while he is in a condition of human frailty? Yet this makes for the honor of the law, 2 Cor. 3. 7, 8. and for the honor of Moses, who brought it to the people, who coming to them with this splendor upon him, was to be received as an angel of God, Gal. 4. 14.

**V. 30.** *{They were afraid}* See Annot. on Exod. 3. 6. If his glory did so affright them, the law might much more terrify them, which in regard of man's transgression, (though in itself holy, just and good, Rom. 7. 12.) wrought wrath, Rom. 3. 15. and that wrath condemnation, 2 Cor. 3. 7, 9. Luk. 1. 12.

**V. 33.** *{A vail}* To hide his face (when he came as an intermediate agent betwixt God and the people, for it may be this glory was not constant all times) because it was too glorious for the eyes of the people: how blind and base is our condition here, when it is not capable of a derivative glimpse of divine glory? and how incomprehensible is God's majesty, when a reflexive ray or beam of his beauty, is too much for mortal men to look upon? See 2 Cor. 3. 13.

**V. 34.** *{Before the Lord}* Which was in the tabernacle or tent of the congregation. What that was, see Annot. on Chap. 33. 7.

*{he took the vail off}* For though he were not capable of God's glory, nor the people of his, yet his glory could be none occasion of terror or amazement, since in comparison of them, it was not so much as the least spark in the chimney to the sun in the firmament.

**V. 35.** *{With him}* That is, with God.

# CHAPTER 35

Much of this chapter is to be found in the twenty-fifth, and the exposition in the Annot. upon it.

**V. 2.** *{six daies}* Chap. 20. 9. Lev. 23. 3. Deut. 5. 13. Luk. 13. 14.

*{an holy day}* Heb. *Holinesse*.

*{put to death}* The Sabbath is so often remembered, and upon such severe penalties imposed, because of the great importance of it to God's glory, and man's good, and the proneness of our corrupt nature, either to forget it, or to profane it.

**V. 3.** *{Kindle no fire}* For any handiwork, though for the framing of the tabernacle: or, (as some expound) for dressing of meat, wherein the Sabbath was more strictly observed, than the first and last day of the feast of unleavened bread, Exod. 12. 16. though (in other respects) they were kept as precisely as the Sabbath: for though (according to our Savior's argument in another case) that which is extraordinary, is usually more solemnly, reverently, and respectively entertained, and there is more cost bestowed upon it, than on that which is ordinary, in this the case is otherwise: for in the ordinary weekly Sabbath, there is a commandment in the decalogue plainly and fully expressed, and by many arguments pressed upon the conscience of the people, all written twice over by God's own writing in the tables of stone, which cannot be said of any of the anniversary feasts: and when the chiefest of them, which was the Passover) light upon the weekly Sabbath, it was to be kept with more restraint, than if it had happened on another day, and then the liberty of dressing meat, or of kindling of a fire, to such a purpose, was to be forborne. Yet because the Sabbath was all the year long, and therefore (in winter as well as in summer) and that in winter little children, sick or weak persons, yea, and such as are reasonably strong, could not comfortably persist, in the celebration of the Sabbath, without fire. It is not probable, that all kind of kindling fire to light a candle, or to dress meat, was forbidden on the Sabbath, but that such a kindling of fire, as the duties of mercy and exigencies of necessity required, was allowed; and though the tabernacle, were of much use for the solemn service of God, and there was very great cause to hasten the work: yet for that God would not give any allowance for any labor upon it, no not so much as for kindling of any fire to heat any tool, or melt any metal; and therefore in this strict prohibition brought in, as an introduction of caution, immediately before the preparation for the tabernacle were presented. See Chap. 16. 23.

**V. 5.** *{Willing heart}* God cares not for that which is given with grudging, 2 Cor. 9. 7.

**V. 10.** *{Wise hearted}* Who was a skillful, an expert artificer, especially for the curious work required for the framing of the tabernacle. See ver. 25, 26, 31, 34, 35. & Chap. 31. 6. & 36. 1, 2.

**V. 11.** *{The Tabernacle}* His tent and by tabernacle is meant the whole, by tent the curious and glorious part of it, and by covering that which was spread upon it to preserve it, yet sometimes the covering of camels' hair over the principal fabric is called a tent, Chap. 36. 14. which is the covering of camels' hair put upon the tabernacle.

**V. 12.** *{The vail of the covering}* Which hung before the mercy seat that it could not be seen. See Note on Chap. 36. 35.

*{incense Altar}* Chap. 30. 1.

**V. 18.** *{Pins}* These were sharpened in the lower part, and fastened in the ground, and they were to keep the curtains from whistling to and fro with the wind: the pillars (which some translators call *pins*) also might be sharpened in like sort, that piercing deep into the ground, they might support the tabernacle with more firmness.

**V. 22.** *{and brought bracelets and ear-rings}* If these were the same who before gave their golden earrings to the making of the golden calf, Chap. 32. 3. they now showed themselves cordial and real converts by this bounty to a better work. If they were not the same, they were more religious towards God, and more wise in honoring him with their substance, and esteeming his service worthy of their most precious jewels.

**V. 26.** *{In wisdom}* That were skillful in spinning.

**V. 28.** *{And spices}* Chap. 30. 23.

**V. 29.** *{by the hands of Moses}* That is, the Lord gave a commandment to Moses concerning the framing of the tabernacle, as it were delivering the work into his hands, to be ordered by his appointment.

**V. 34.** *{may teach}* So cunning and skillful as to be masters of their art, and able to instruct and teach others.

**V. 35.** *{A vail of}* That was the inward veil of the tabernacle, dividing betwixt the holy of holies, (where the ark was) and the other part called the sanctuary.

# CHAPTER 36

**Verse 1.** *{ALL manner}* Chap. 35. 31.

*{Sanctuary}* By the sanctuary, he means here all the tabernacle.

**V. 2.** *{Called Bezaleel}* Chap. 31. 2. They were endowed with gifts before, but that qualification did not warrant them to enter upon the public work, without a peculiar calling of authority. See Exod. 18. 25. Acts 6. 3.

**V. 3.** *{every morning}* Heb. *in morning in morning*: That is, morning by morning, or every morning. By morning some conceive is understood the whole day artificial, as Gen. Chap. 1. without any difference or distinction of hours: but in regard of the great forwardness of the people, it is like the former part of the day is meant.

**V. 6.** *{Neither man nor woman}* Both sexes were employed in preparing the tabernacle: the women in carding and spinning colored wool, and men in weaving their work into a web of cloth, and other works.

*{restrained from bringing}* Their liberality condemns the backwardness of many now either to build an house to the Lord, or to uphold and repair it, what it has been built by others: and this restraint shows and blames their fault, who think there cannot be too much bestowed on the outward magnificence and ornament of temples or churches.

**V. 8.** *{Cherubims}* Which were little pictures with wings in the form of children. See Annot. on Exod. 25. 18.

**V. 10.** *{Fifty loops}* Chap. 26. 10.

**V. 14.** *{Tent over the Tabernacle}* The tabernacle was a tent, and is so called, ver. 18. but this tent was a coarser covering, to keep it fair from offense of foul weather. See Annot. on Chap. 35. 11.

**V. 19.** *{Above that}* There were divers coverings of the most curious tabernacle, one above another, to preserve it from the annoyance of rain, and stormy seasons. See on ver. 14. and on Chap. 26. 7.

**V. 20.** *{Standing up}* To bear up the curtains of the tabernacle.

**V. 27.** *{Westward}* Or, *toward the sea*, which was the sea called Mediterranean westward from Jerusalem.

**V. 29.** *{coupled}* Heb. *Twined*.

**V. 30.** *{every board}* Heb. *two sockets under one board*.

**V. 33.** *{middle bar}* Of this bar there is no mention, in any precedent description of the tabernacle; it seems to be a bar thrust through the middle of the boards; there were other bars on the outside, which were of use both to unite and strengthen them.

**V. 35.** *{a vail of blue}* This was the inward veil of the tabernacle betwixt the sanctuary and the holiest of all, where the ark was placed.

*{Cherubims}* See Annot. on ver. 8.

**V. 37.** *{hanging for}* Which was betwixt the court and the sanctuary.

*{needle work}* Heb. *The work of a needle work*, or *embroiderer*.

**V. 38.** *{Overlaid their Chapiters}* That is, their heads, and knobs which were covered with golden plates, that nothing outwardly appeared on them but gold: but the pillars though they had gold upon them, were not wholly covered with it, but had, as it were, girdles, or hoops of gold here and there; whereby they were rather adorned, than covered; for between these golden girdles, or couples, the wood was seen. It may seem (by Chap. 26. ver. 37.) that the pillars themselves were overlaid with gold; but in this 36. chapter the particulars are more distinctly set down, than in the 26. and therefore that place is to be expounded by this, as in the present Annot. which is confirmed, Chap. 38. 17.

# CHAPTER 37

Many of the particulars here mentioned are brought in before, Chap. 25. and Chap. 39. and there expounded.

**V. 1.** *{the Ark}* Chap. 25. 10.

**V. 2.** *{Crown of gold}* See Annot. on Exod. 30. 3.

**V. 6.** *{Mercy seat}* See Annot. on Chap. 25. 17.

**V. 7.** *{Cherubims}* See on Chap. 25. 18. & 36. 8.

**V. 8.** *{on the end}* Or, *out of the end*.

*{on the}* Or, *out of the*, &c. Of the selfsame matter that the mercy seat was.

**V. 16.** *{Dishes}* Chap. 25. 29.

*{to cover}* Or, *to pour out withal*. The Hebrew word signifies *to cover*, and to pour out: and in both senses, these vessels called bowls, or covers might be of use: for they might serve both to cover the showbread, and to pour out the drink offering.

**V. 17.** *{Candlestick}* Chap. 25. 31.

**V. 29.** *{holy anointing oil}* Chap. 30. 35.

# CHAPTER 38

**Verse 1.** *{He made}* Chap. 27. 1.

**V. 4.** *{unto the midst}* So that the gridiron, or grate, was half so high as the altar, and stood within it.

**V. 8.** *{Looking-glasses}* Or, *Brasen glasses*. Rab. Kimchi says, that women brought their *looking* glasses, which were of brass, or fine metal, and offered them freely unto the use of the tabernacle. The words in the original, and other languages, have nothing in them that signifies glass, but only looking or *representing the look*, or *to the looks*, which may be done in many sorts of metals; and for brass in particular, it was the manner (not only among the Jews, but other nations) for women to dress themselves by setting a piece of shining brass, or some other piece of bright burnished metal before their faces; as the manner with us, and many others is to use steeled glasses. See Plin. *Nat. Hist.* lib. 33. cap. 9. These women, (notwithstanding their former fashion to trim themselves by them) they willingly gave up to the service of the sanctuary, preferring therein God's glory before their own gracefulness.

*{assembling}* Their assembling to give up their desirable things to the service of God, was (according to the original reading) *by troops*; so forward and freehearted were they in their offerings, that they seemed to make haste, and as it were to strive who should come soonest with their gift.

**V. 13.** *{East-side Eastward}* Chap. 27. 13.

**V. 21.** *{Sum of the Tabernacle}* As an inventory of the particular furniture of the tabernacle, to be kept by the Levites, and at the cost which was bestowed upon it by the oblations of the people.

*{Levites}* That the Levites might have the charge thereof, and minister in the same, as Eleazar and Ithamar, Num. 3. 4.

**V. 24.** *{all the gold}* 1 Chr. 22. 14.

**V. 25.** *{an hundred talents}* Every talent took up in weight and value three thousand shekels. See Annot. on Gen. 23. 15. and on Exod. 30. 13.

**V. 26.** *{six hundred thousand}* Chap. 12. 37. Numb. 12. 46. Exod. 30. 13.

*{half a shekel}* The rate set upon the people was for the offering of silver, ver. 25. but for the gold, ver. 24. there is no law, that sets any rate upon them. The gold therefore was freely given, the silver exacted.

**V. 27.** *{an hundred sockets}* Whereof forty were on the south side, forty on the north, sixteen on the west, and four on the east, under the four pillars, divided the sanctuary from the holy of holies. See Chap. 26. 19, 32.

**V. 29.** *{talents}* See Annot. on Chap. 25. 39.

# CHAPTER 39

Much of the contents of this chapter, is before at Chap. 28. and there expounded.

**Ver. 1.** *{And made}* See Chap. 31. 10.

**V. 6.** *{Onyx stones}* Chap. 28. 9.

**V. 7.** *{memorial}* Chap. 28. 12.

**V. 10.** *{four rowes}* See Annot. on Chap. 28. 17.

**V. 23.** *{habergeon}* See Chap. 28. 32.

**V. 25.** *{Bells}* Chap. 28. 33.

**V. 30.** *{Holiness}* Chap. 28. 36.

**V. 31.** *{As the Lord commanded}* See Chap. 40. 32.

**V. 33.** *{Vail of a covering}* So called, because it hung before the mercy seat, and covered it from sight of such as came into the sanctuary, Chap. 35. 12.

**V. 37.** *{Set in order}* Or *which Aaron dressed and refreshed with oil every morning*, Chap. 30. 7.

**V. 38.** *{sweet incense}* Heb. *the incense of sweet Spices*.

**V. 42.** *{According to all}* Signifying, that in God's matters man may neither add nor diminish. Chap. 25. 40. 2 Chron. 28. 19.

**V. 43.** *{Blessed them}* Whereby is not meant (as some take it) the consecration of the tabernacle, and of the things that appertained to it, for that is done in the next chapter: but the blessing of the people for their forwardness and bounty, for the building and furnishing of the tabernacle, and of the artificers for their care and exactness in the workmanship of every particular, and his blessing of them, was praising of them for what they did; and praying for them, that God would bless them for his sanctuary, for their goodwill and freeheartedness to his sanctuary.

# CHAPTER 40

**Verse 2.** *{The first day}* Of the first month, and the second year after their departure out of Egypt, ver. 17. after that Moses had the second time been forty days and forty nights in the mount, he came down, and caused this work to be done: which being finished, was set up in the month called Abib, which contains part of March, and part of April.

*{Tabernacle of the tent}* See Annot. on ver. 6.

*{of the Congregation}* Of meeting of the priests and Levites, and people, according to the degrees of their approaches, for the high priests had admittance where other priests had not; and the priests in respect of place, had a preeminence above the Levites, and they above the people who assembled in the court of the tabernacle; yet so, as they were all accounted of one assembly, where God by his especial presence, met with his assembled servants. See Annot. on Chap. 29. 42, 43.

**V. 4.** *{Set in order upon it}* Which (every week) were to be renewed, and set in order. See Annot. on Chap. 25. 30.

**V. 5.** *{Hanging}* This hanging or veil was betwixt the sanctuary and the court.

**V. 6.** *{Tabernacle of the Tent}* When the name tabernacle, and tent, are thus set down, (though they be so far the same, that you may call the tent a tabernacle, and the tabernacle a tent) the one may be taken for the curious piece of work, which was so richly, and so gloriously framed; the other for the covering that was upon it, ver. 10. to preserve it, as a type of God's providence and protection of his church, Isai. 4. 6. See ver. 19.

**V. 9.** *{anoint the Tabernacle}* See Lev. 8. 10.

**V. 10.** *{The Altar}* See Lev. 8. 11.

*{most holy}* Heb. *holiness of holinesses*.

**V. 15.** *{everlasting}* So that their children succeeding, should not need to be anointed again, but approved in their office successively, by virtue of the first unction: See on Chap. 29. 7, 29. Lev. 4. 3. 2 Mach. 1. 10. and though only the high priest were particularly anointed in his personal succession; yet the priesthood may be called everlasting in that sense, that other Levitical ordinances are said to last forever. See Chap. 12. 14.

*{second year}* That is, after they came out of Egypt, Num. 7. 1. so that they continued in the wilderness a whole year almost before this tabernacle was set up, and it was set up seven months after Moses came with his directions for it, from the mount: for he came down thence the twenty-eighth day of the fifth month after their departure out of Egypt; from whence to the first day of the first month of the second year, are seven months, which with the five months before) make up a whole year, with which ends the story of the book of Exodus. See on ver. 38.

**V. 20.** *{Testimony}* See Annot. on Exod. 16. 34.

**V. 21.** *{Vail of the covering}* See Annot. on Chap. 35. 12.

**V. 22.** *{Table}* That is, the table of the showbread.

**V. 23.** *{in order}* See Annot. on Chap. 25. 30.

**V. 27.** *{he burnt sweet incense thereon}* Moses for the present did that, which when the tabernacle was finished, and the worship of it established in a settled course, belonged to the priest.

**V. 28.** *{door of}* That is, between the sanctuary and the court.

**V. 29.** *{as the Lord commanded}* At the 19 verse.

**V. 32.** *{they washed}* Exod. 30. 20, 21.

*{as the Lord commanded}* These words in this chapter, are repeated many times, ten times Chap. 39. and eight times in this chapter; to note what warrant Moses had for his curious work, and how punctual he was in performance of God's command; and in the exact repetition of so many particulars, he shows that his history is composed not for delight, but for truth's sake, since these particulars have no allurement in them, to make a wise man willing so often, either by his pen to write them, or to read them when they are written, being many of them, (saving for God's command) matters of little moment.

**V. 34.** *{then a cloud}* The pillar of the cloud, which was before upon the former tabernacle, now (by a new miracle) removed to the new tabernacle; so did the pillar of fire, shining with greater glory than before. See Levit. 16. 2. Num. 9. 15. 1 King. 8. 10. Isai. 6. 4. Rev. 15. 8.

**V. 35.** *{not able}* By reason of an astonishing reverence; and it may be also, the brightness of the glory, might be some impediment to his sight for a time; this was extraordinary, for if it had been usual, it would have hindered the holy ministration in that place. See 1 Kings 8. 11.

**V. 36.** *{Went}* Heb. *journeyed*. Num. 9. 17, 23.

**V. 38.** *{For the cloud}* Thus the presence of God preserved and guided them, until they came to the promised land. Numb. 9. 15, 16.

*{fire was on it}* That is, was over it, so as to give light, not to cause any burning: and while the cloud stayed, the people stirred not; when it removed, they removed also, and went that way whereto it guided them. Thus is the book of Exodus at an end. See Annot. on ver. 17.
